- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Dido, leader of Carthage (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Carthage
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Atlas Elephant, Replacing Horseman (Requires technology: Trade)
        - Unique Unit: Quinquereme, Replacing Trireme (Requires technology: Philosophy)
        - Unique Building: Great Cothon, Replacing Chartered Company (Requires technology: Currency)
        - Civilization Ability: +100 Gold and +25 Science when your all-time highest tradeable quantity of a Luxury increases, and +5 XP to Gold purchased Units, both scaling with Era. +100% Resource Diversity on Trade Routes. Starts with an Asamu instead of a Settler.
      - Leader: Dido
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Robotics: An important technology for the Space Race, Robotics allows you to build the Spaceship Factory, a building which speeds the construction of Spaceship parts. It also allows you to build the Missile Cruiser, a Naval Unit capable of carrying 3 Missiles and spotting enemy Submarines.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics
      - Stealth: Allows you to build the Stealth Bomber, the most powerful Ranged Unit in the game.
        Completing it would unlock: Globalization, Nuclear Fusion
      - The Internet: Increases Tourism output of all cities by 50%, helping you advance toward a Cultural Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization
      
      ## Policies
      - Martial Spirit (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Martial Spirit: +25% Combat Strength when attacking in melee for 50 turns, scaling with game speed. War Weariness reduced by 25%, and Razing Speed is doubled.
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Military-Industrial Complex (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Military-Industrial Complex: -33% Gold cost of purchasing units. +3 Science from Defense Buildings, Citadels, Forts, and Unique Improvements.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - Police State (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Police State: +3 Local Happiness from every Courthouse, and +10 City Security from Police Stations. +100% Production towards Courthouses and Police Stations.
      - Total War (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Total War: +25% Production when building Land Units. Warscore increases 25% more quickly, and it is 25% easier to bully City-States.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Lightning Warfare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lightning Warfare: +3 Movement for Great Generals. Gunpowder units gain +15% attack and ignore enemy ZOC; Armored units gain +15% attack and +1 Movement.
      - Elite Forces (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Elite Forces: Newly created Military Units receive +15 Experience. Military Units gain 50% more Experience from combat.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Dominance (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Melee Units gain the Endurance Promotion (Heal 15 HP after defeating an Enemy Unit).
        - * +1 Production in every City for every 10 Military Units in Empire.
        - * +5 Military Supply.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

        ## Persona

        - Rationale: Fine-tuning persona for final 18 turns. VictoryCompetitiveness maximized to 10 for science race desperation. Boldness reduced to absolute minimum (1) - no risks, pure defense. WarBias stays at 1 to avoid any offensive wars. GuardedBias increased to 9 reflecting extreme defensive mentality against all threats. MinorCivWarBias reduced to 1 - no city-state conflicts to distract from spaceship race. All other values unchanged for consistency.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Robotics
        - Rationale: Telecommunications completes in 4 turns. Robotics is ESSENTIAL for the spaceship race - unlocks Spaceship Factory which provides massive production bonuses for spaceship parts. China leads with 33% completion (2 parts). Without Spaceship Factory, we cannot catch up. This is the critical infrastructure technology for finishing spaceship components in the final 12 turns after Robotics completes.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: None (Policy)
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Jerusalem
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 45
        - Rationale: Reducing Jerusalem relationship from 100 to 40. Iroquois has insurmountable 362 influence lead with only 8 turns to World Congress. Cannot overcome this gap without massive gold investment better spent on Tyre. Maintaining moderate positive to keep quest opportunities open for future.
        - UpdatedTurn: 205
        
        ### Shedet
        - Public: -60
        - Private: -55
        - Rationale: Shedet HOSTILE: America's ally declared war on us. America stole this with Great Diplomat (163 vs our -60). -60/-55 signals lost cause, focus military solution only. Spies and envoys should not waste time here - go to Kabul/Antwerp instead.
        - UpdatedTurn: 227
        
        ### Abernethy

        - Private: -45

        - UpdatedTurn: 251
        
        ### Prague

        - UpdatedTurn: 299
        
        ### Wittenberg

Current 70/65 relationship provides no diplomatic benefit while active. Resetting to 0/0 redirects AI attention and resources to contestable city-states like Vancouver or defending La Venta against Open Door threat.

        ### Tyre

Resetting to 0/0 to focus on defensive spy operations against China/America instead of unwinnable city-state contests.

        - Public: 0

Maximum hostility (-90/-90) ensures AI prepares for active war, maintains nuclear retaliation readiness, and positions all defenses immediately.
        - UpdatedTurn: 469
        
        ### China
        - Public: 100

        - Rationale: China: Defensive Pact ally (47 turns remaining). Only protection against American nuclear attack. 100/100 mandatory to maintain alliance despite -999 ideology penalty. Their 16,820 military strength and nuclear deterrent is critical for survival now that American war is active.
        - UpdatedTurn: 469
        
        ### Songhai
        - Public: 100

        - Rationale: Songhai: Frontline ally against America, shares Autocracy ideology. America has been nuking their cities (Sukhothai). Maximum friendship for solidarity and military coordination now that American war has begun.

        ### Siam

        - Rationale: Polynesia: They have Order ideology (-999), but are distant and not immediate threat. Setting moderate hostility to focus resources on America/Iroquois. They fear our might and are Guarded, suggesting limited offensive capacity against us.
        - UpdatedTurn: 469
        
        ### The Iroquois
        - Public: -50

        - Rationale: Iroquois peace treaty expires in ~2-3 turns. Increasing hostility from -30/-25 to -50/-45 prepares AI for potential border conflict without provoking premature attack. They have Defensive Pact with America and Order ideology - definite threat once peace expires.

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 44
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 10 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Global Peace Accords:
            - Description: Unit maintenance costs are raised by 25% Gold. 2.0x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 0.5x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Seoul:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Order:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: 484
          - Open Door: Wittenberg:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: Songhai:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: La Venta:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Vancouver:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Manila:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: China:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 462
          - American Sphere of Influence: Shedet:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Polynesian Sphere of Influence: Kabul:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Global Hegemony:
            - Description: Designate a player that could achieve Global Hegemony. If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory. The number of Delegates needed depends on the number of Civilizations and City-States in the game, and can be seen on the World Congress screen.
              If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates to use in future sessions.
              May not be proposed by a Civilization. Automatically proposed at regular intervals once a World Ideology Resolution is active.
            - Proposer: Unknown

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Dido, leader of Carthage (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Carthage
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Atlas Elephant, Replacing Horseman (Requires technology: Trade)
        - Unique Unit: Quinquereme, Replacing Trireme (Requires technology: Philosophy)
        - Unique Building: Great Cothon, Replacing Chartered Company (Requires technology: Currency)
        - Civilization Ability: +100 Gold and +25 Science when your all-time highest tradeable quantity of a Luxury increases, and +5 XP to Gold purchased Units, both scaling with Era. +100% Resource Diversity on Trade Routes. Starts with an Asamu instead of a Settler.
      - Leader: Dido
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Robotics: An important technology for the Space Race, Robotics allows you to build the Spaceship Factory, a building which speeds the construction of Spaceship parts. It also allows you to build the Missile Cruiser, a Naval Unit capable of carrying 3 Missiles and spotting enemy Submarines.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics
      - Stealth: Allows you to build the Stealth Bomber, the most powerful Ranged Unit in the game.
        Completing it would unlock: Globalization, Nuclear Fusion
      - The Internet: Increases Tourism output of all cities by 50%, helping you advance toward a Cultural Victory.
        Completing it would unlock: Nanotechnology, Particle Physics, Globalization
      
      ## Policies
      - Martial Spirit (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Martial Spirit: +25% Combat Strength when attacking in melee for 50 turns, scaling with game speed. War Weariness reduced by 25%, and Razing Speed is doubled.
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Military-Industrial Complex (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Military-Industrial Complex: -33% Gold cost of purchasing units. +3 Science from Defense Buildings, Citadels, Forts, and Unique Improvements.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - Police State (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Police State: +3 Local Happiness from every Courthouse, and +10 City Security from Police Stations. +100% Production towards Courthouses and Police Stations.
      - Total War (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Total War: +25% Production when building Land Units. Warscore increases 25% more quickly, and it is 25% easier to bully City-States.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Lightning Warfare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lightning Warfare: +3 Movement for Great Generals. Gunpowder units gain +15% attack and ignore enemy ZOC; Armored units gain +15% attack and +1 Movement.
      - Elite Forces (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Elite Forces: Newly created Military Units receive +15 Experience. Military Units gain 50% more Experience from combat.
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Dominance (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * All Melee Units gain the Endurance Promotion (Heal 15 HP after defeating an Enemy Unit).
        - * +1 Production in every City for every 10 Military Units in Empire.
        - * +5 Military Supply.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Rationale: Selecting Mare Nostrum as the next policy tenet. As Carthage, all 9 of our cities are coastal with extensive water resource improvements (over 20 fish, plus whales, pearls, etc.). Mare Nostrum provides +2 Production and Culture to all fishing boats and offshore platforms, giving an immediate and massive empire-wide boost to culture and production to help maximize our final score in the remaining 15 turns.
        - Policy: Mare Nostrum
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Robotics
        - Rationale: Keeping Robotics as our next research goal. It is the logical progression from Telecommunications and unlocks the Spaceship Factory, which is the most critical infrastructure for science and late-game production, ensuring we continue to advance our technology score.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Private: -20
        - Rationale: The peace treaty with the Iroquois has expired this turn. While we have denounced each other, they are militarily very weak (3.7k strength) and we want to avoid getting dragged into a needless war that would disrupt our focus on population growth and technology. Setting our stance to a cautious but less hostile -20/-20 minimizes the chance of our AI provoking them into an attack.
        - TargetID: 5
        - Public: -20
    - #3:

          - UseNuke: 95

      - rationale: With only 15 turns remaining, we must shift our focus to maximizing our final score for a Time Victory. We drastically increase Growth (85) and Happiness (70) to boost population score, and Culture (75) to unlock our next policy. Gold (85) is prioritized to address our severe budget deficit and avoid research penalties. Mobilization is reduced to 30 because we are at our military supply limit and further unit production would only worsen our gold deficit. Production is slightly lowered to 70 to balance with food and gold tiles. Defensive and scientific priorities remain at maximum.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Assessing Game Status**
            
            The game is nearing its end, with only 15 turns left out of 500. My current focus is on evaluating our progress towards the primary objective: achieving victory or securing the highest score if victory isn't possible. I'm examining the immediate situation to strategize for these final turns.
            
            **Evaluating Victory Conditions**
            
            My current analysis reveals that a Domination Victory is unlikely, as neither I nor our primary rivals hold enough capitals. The Science Victory is also slipping away; China is significantly ahead with parts construction, and our own research and production timelines make a last-minute launch improbable within the remaining 15 turns.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Paths**
            
            I'm now zeroing in on the Diplomatic Victory. With the World Congress voting imminent, I'm scrutinizing delegate counts, particularly America's strong position, and assessing our own potential to sway city-states, as that seems to be our most viable path to securing enough votes for victory.
            
            **Re-evaluating Diplomatic Leverage**
            
            My focus has shifted to delegate counts. I see America holds significant sway with numerous city-state alliances. I'm scrutinizing influence levels, especially regarding Carthage and China's potential to secure key city-states, as this now seems critical to my diplomatic victory pursuit.
            
            **Calculating Final Score**
            
            I'm recalculating our path to victory. While a diplomatic win seemed likely, our current score is critically close to America's. I'm now focused on boosting our score through population growth and leveraging our tech advantage, aiming to secure second place if outright victory proves impossible.
            
            **Investigating Score Discrepancies**
            
            I'm puzzled by America's higher score despite our larger population and territory. I'm re-examining our tech progress, specifically the slight delay in Telecommunications research, and considering the strategic value of Robotics versus Stealth for our endgame.
            
            **Analyzing Trade Routes**
            
            My focus has shifted to our dire financial situation. I'm scrutinizing outgoing trade routes and their current gold generation, realizing our negative per turn income is severely impacting our ability to build and research effectively, thus jeopardizing any remaining score-boosting initiatives.
            
            **Investigating Trade Route Costs**
            
            I'm digging into our gold expenditure, specifically the disconnect between individual city gold generation and the overall negative per turn income. I need to understand if trade route gold is being offset by other expenses, or if there's a misunderstanding of the "GoldPerTurn" metric itself.
            
            **Understanding Gold Deficit**
            
            I've identified the core issue: astronomical unit maintenance costs, amplified by the Global Peace Accords, are completely overwhelming positive city income. This deficit is now critically impacting our science output, even though the raw number still appears high. I'm exploring immediate solutions to this financial crisis.
            
            **Adjusting Policy Focus**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our policy choices given the limited remaining turns. Since our primary goal is now score maximization rather than a specific victory type, I'm looking at policies that might boost our gold generation or overall score, even if we won't see the full benefit.
            
            **Reviewing Policy Choices**
            
            I'm reviewing our extensive policy options given the endgame. Considering our current diplomatic standing, "Syndicalism" appears promising for increased production, while "Mare Nostrum" could bolster vital resources. I'm weighing these against continuing down other policy branches for potential score benefits.
            
            **Adopting Policy Choice**
            
            I've decided to adopt "Mare Nostrum" as our next policy. Given our coastal cities and abundant fishing resources, the production and culture bonuses will significantly boost our empire's output and cultural generation in these final turns, aligning with our score maximization objective.
            
            **Analyzing City-State Alliances**
            
            I'm examining our recent diplomatic shifts, particularly the dramatic increase in friendship with Antwerp. I'm trying to pinpoint the exact cause of this sudden boost in influence and understand how it might impact our delegate count moving forward, especially in light of China's strong alliance there.
            
            **Securing Delegates**
            
            I've identified Kabul as a critical city-state. It's only 3 influence away from an alliance, which would grant us a vital delegate. I'm ensuring our relationship remains a priority to secure this advantage and am now assessing other high-influence city-states to maximize our delegate count.
            
            **Investigating Diplomatic Entanglements**
            
            I'm re-examining our diplomatic standing, particularly our current "Neutral" status with Jerusalem despite a recent gift. This has me scrutinizing America's influence there and exploring how our other relationships, like the "Denounced Them" status with the Iroquois, might inadvertently affect our delegate acquisition and overall standing in these critical final turns.
            
            **Investigating Peace Treaty Expiration**
            
            My immediate concern is the Iroquois peace treaty, which has just expired. This poses a significant threat, especially given their defensive pact with America. I'm now urgently checking our own defensive pacts, particularly with China, to prepare for potential escalations in these final turns.
            
            **Investigating Defensive Pact Status**
            
            I'm trying to reconcile conflicting information regarding our defensive pacts. It appears the crucial pact with China is no longer active, a critical oversight with the Iroquois peace treaty expired and them allied with America. I'm urgently assessing our overall defensive posture.
            
            **Clarifying War Status**
            
            My current analysis reveals a critical misinterpretation: while the Iroquois peace treaty expired and we are denounced by America, we are not currently at war with America. This misunderstanding stemmed from observing America's war with China, but our own status with America is one of hostility, not active conflict.
            
            **Assessing Threat Levels**
            
            I'm relieved to confirm we are at peace with America, despite recent diplomatic tensions and their immense military strength. The expiring Iroquois treaty and their defensive pact with America are still concerning, as America could declare war on us at any moment.
            
            **Analyzing Bloc Strengths**
            
            I'm assessing the global power balance. Our bloc, bolstered by Carthage and Songhai, holds a significant military advantage over America's alliance. However, America's individual strength, combined with their delegate and tech leads, remains a substantial challenge that I'm now strategizing to overcome.
            
            **Forming Defensive Pacts**
            
            I'm focusing on solidifying our alliances. With China and Songhai at maximum relationship, I'm ensuring defensive pacts are being pursued automatically by the AI, anticipating potential escalations. I'm also investigating Carthage's current production, as its focus on research seems odd given our tech trajectory and extensive building inventory.
            
            **Boosting Production Power**
            
            My current focus is on the impending completion of numerous power plants across our cities. This surge in production will dramatically enhance our ability to construct endgame objectives or further boost our score.
            
            **Increasing Population Growth**
            
            My current analysis of score calculation reveals population growth as a key driver. I'm consequently prioritizing a significant increase in our "Growth" flavor, aiming for 80, while simultaneously ensuring our cities are not experiencing starvation and can sustain this heightened growth by reallocating citizens to food tiles.
            
            **Boosting Gold Generation**
            
            My focus has shifted to immediate financial recovery. To address the critical gold deficit and its impact on our science output, I'm prioritizing an increase in the "Gold" flavor to 80. This should incentivize AI to focus on gold-generating tiles and buildings, hopefully returning our per-turn income to positive territory.
            
            **Boosting Culture Generation**
            
            I'm now prioritizing a significant increase in our "Culture" flavor to 60. This aims to accelerate policy adoption, specifically targeting "Mare Nostrum" to secure a substantial score boost and a valuable tenet bonus before the game concludes.
            
            **Revising Unit Production**
            
            I'm adjusting our military focus. Given our current unit count and maintenance costs, further unit production is detrimental to our gold per turn. I'm significantly reducing the "Mobilization" flavor to 30, prioritizing growth and other score-enhancing factors.
            
            **Adjusting Flavor Prioritization**
            
            I'm reallocating our strategic focus, prioritizing gold generation to combat deficits and boost our score, while also increasing culture for faster policy adoption. Happiness levels are also being addressed to enhance growth and scoring potential.
            
            **Increasing AI Competitiveness**
            
            My current focus is on influencing the AI's city-state priorities. While I cannot directly set relationships with city-states like Kabul, I can increase `MinorCivCompetitiveness`. This should encourage more aggressive AI competition for city-state alliances. Given our zero gold, this is a safe gamble, and I'm also noting our active spy operations.
            
            **Deploying Spies Strategically**
            
            I'm perplexed by our current spy deployments. With America trailing in technology, I'm questioning the purpose of our agents there. I'm investigating the possibility of reassigning them to city-states to bolster delegate counts, as our tactical AI handles spy assignments and I need to understand the reasoning behind their current positioning to optimize for score.
            
            **Adjusting Diplomatic Stance**
            
            I'm fine-tuning our relationships to mitigate war risks. My current assessment involves slightly de-escalating hostility with the Iroquois, while maintaining a strictly defensive posture towards the overwhelmingly powerful America. China remains our steadfast ally.
            
            **Refining Diplomatic Relations**
            
            I'm reviewing our existing diplomatic standings. Most relationships, including those with China, Songhai, and Siam, are already optimized at Public and Private 100, which is ideal for our current defensive stance. Polynesia's slightly negative values remain acceptable. My persona values are also well-suited for survival.
            
            **Finalizing Policy and Tech**
            
            I've confirmed "Mare Nostrum" as the optimal policy for maximizing production and culture in these final turns. Concurrently, I've locked in "Robotics" as our next research target, recognizing its critical role in late-game infrastructure and the spaceship race, even if completion is improbable.
            
            **Finalizing Game Strategy**
            
            I've locked in "Mare Nostrum" and "Robotics," adjusted our Iroquois relationship to minimize conflict, and rebalanced flavors to prioritize growth, gold, culture, and happiness while reducing unit maintenance costs and focusing on defense and science for maximum score potential in these final turns.

            - Public: -20
          - providerExecuted: undefined
